Implementation
Change logImplementation requirements

GTM Process

Read the progression summary first, then the field timing. Before Stage marks an exit gate; On entry is required for that transition. System-maintained fields are stamped by automation, not filled in by the seller. Current inputs describe the captured inventory; proposed inputs describe the target change.

Stage specification

Opportunity creation

Capture source attribution and the originating Contact; record SAL on opportunity creation, or SAL and SQL for seller-qualified direct entry to Stage 1.

Owner · Sales · Marketing · Partners · BDRs · Forecast: Pre-pipeline · Pipeline for direct Stage 1

Create with a valid origin

Resolve the Account and originating Contact, required core fields and source-dependent sourcing reference. Inbound intake starts at Stage 0; only the assigned seller-self-qualified path can start at Stage 1. Seller assignment and source attribution are separate.

Update Stage

  1. Choose the destination stage and review the prepopulated evidence.
  2. Complete missing inputs for every gate crossed; route protected technical or approval evidence to its responsible owner.
  3. Save only when validation passes. Cancellation or failure leaves the stage and entry dates unchanged.
Complete progression rule

Provide an Update Stage screen flow that prepopulates existing evidence and presents the missing required inputs for the requested destination and every prerequisite gate crossed. Identify each missing field, its requirement and where to complete it. Respect field permissions; for technical evidence, related-record work or approval outside the user’s authority, identify the responsible owner and next action. Validate all evidence before committing the stage and associated evidence together; a failed save or cancellation must not advance the stage or stamp entry dates. Keep equivalent gates on direct edits, imports, integrations and automation. Fallback errors must name the missing inputs and provide navigation where the interface supports it. Preserve valid existing values, source-specific qualification, loss-specific rules, the Stage 6 → 5 correction path and booked locks.

Salesforce flow configuration

Collect inputs in flow variables before the final confirmation. Re-read the current record and recheck permissions and prerequisite gates at save. Perform the final evidence, stage and timestamp writes in one transaction; route faults through Roll Back Records before displaying an error screen. Do not place a Screen, Pause or other transaction boundary between dependent writes. Cancelling a later screen cannot undo changes already committed by an earlier transaction. Keep notifications and integration effects after successful commit and deduplicate retries.

Salesforce transaction guidance

Opportunity SourcePicklist (source inventory; control not verified)

Proposed choices: Direct / BDR / Marketing / Partner.

Historical choices and migration

Current: Direct, Channel Partners, Server Vendors, BDR, Cloud, GSI, Marketing. Crosswalk validated partner-origin histories to Partner; retain original values, source evidence and Sourcing Partner. Resolve ambiguous records before migration. Partner identity/classification remains on the partner relationship; Route to Market stays separate.

Conversion ContactRelated records

Select/link an existing Salesforce Contact; no new buying-role choice is required. Exact originating-contact relationship mapping must be confirmed.

Associates existing Contacts with the opportunity and records their buying roles.Extend trackingAt creation · originating Contact associationCapture a resolved originating Contact at creation for conversion tracking; maintain buying roles through discovery and require at least one assigned Opportunity Contact Role before Stage 2.
Full rule

These are separate checks. Reuse a verified existing originating/primary Contact relationship or a designated Contact Role; confirm the exact relationship and creation transaction before configuration. Primary Contact remains optional as a committee designation unless it is the approved conversion-link mapping. Do not require every buying-committee member at creation. Use an explicitly designated originating/conversion Contact linked through Opportunity Contact Roles; reuse a supported existing primary/originating Contact relationship before adding storage. Require a resolved Contact association for creation/conversion tracking and before Stage 1; do not update every buying-committee member or infer the person from an account alone. The account is the linked Contact account in the same deal context; flag conflicting associations for review. Extend the existing Contact lifecycle and Account Lifecycle Stage (ALS) mappings rather than creating parallel Contact/Account lifecycle fields. Show per-contact conversion and related-opportunity evidence on the Account; preserve its current lifecycle semantics and customer states until the rollup mapping is approved. One new Opportunity Conversion Lifecycle field shows SAL/SQL for this opportunity; retain it as the last conversion milestone after discovery, separate from sales Stage.

Opportunity Conversion LifecyclePicklist · system-maintained · proposed
Proposed inputs

Proposed field; no current input control. Use the proposed type shown above.

Proposed inputs
  • SAL
  • SQL
No existing field confirmed; proposed capture.New fieldSystem-maintained · SAL at creation / SQL at qualified pipeline entryNew Opportunity-level conversion milestone: SAL on creation; SQL at first qualified pipeline entry, including permitted skips and seller-direct Stage 1 creation.
Full rule

Separate from sales Stage and existing Contact/Account lifecycle. Retain source, qualification channel (MAP / PAX.AI / seller / partner / BDR), event timestamp, actor/system, Contact, Account and Opportunity relationship in conversion history. Reuse suitable existing lifecycle history/event storage, extending only missing data. Record MQL or AQL at Contact qualification; SAL at opportunity creation; accepted/rejected decision separately; SQL on first qualified entry to Stages 1–8, including permitted skips. Preserve every episode for multiple opportunities or requalification and deduplicate retries with the source event/episode ID. Late MQL/AQL messages, stage regression and a new Stage 0 opportunity must not downgrade a Contact or Account already further through its lifecycle. Keep opportunity-level events so separate deals remain reportable. Report MQL→SAL→SQL and AQL→SAL→SQL as separate cohorts, including rejected/pending SALs and elapsed time. Seller-direct SQLs remain a separate cohort; never inflate inbound conversion by fabricating MQL/AQL history.

Ongoing activities

  • Conversion tracking
  • Partner contribution

Automations and validations

  • In the creation action, resolve the originating Contact before inserting the Opportunity, then create or link the designated Contact relationship in the same controlled transaction. If using Opportunity Contact Roles, the parent Opportunity ID must exist before its child role record can be created. Do not implement a before-insert child-existence check that makes valid creation impossible. Roll back failed parent/link creation and retain equivalent controls for integration paths. Access rules.
  • Configure the numbered sales Stage values with Open type for 0–6, Closed/Won for 7–8, and Closed/Lost for the lost outcome. IsClosed / IsWon follow Stage configuration; do not create duplicate checkbox fields. Stage 6 customer selection is not booking. Keep the existing administrative outcomes and standard forecasting configuration separately mapped; Forecast Category (Sales) is the distinct business field described here. Access rules.
  • Protect accepted partner-originated Opportunity Source and Sourcing Partner from ordinary AE or overlay edits. Corrections require an explicitly authorized RevOps / Partner Ops action with evidence, reason, prior/new values, actor and timestamp. This is separate from Partner Contribution (Mgt). Apply the restriction to UI, imports and integrations. On creation or activation of a partner association, resolve the partner Account’s Primary DDN Partner Account Manager and Partner Technical Lead. Add each active user to the opportunity team only when the partner AND approved geography/territory coverage match. Send missing or mismatched coverage to Partner Ops; do not silently broaden access. Deduplicate membership across associations, and reconcile owner changes, coverage transfers, deactivation and expiry using the recorded grant reason. Remove only access derived from the changed assignment; preserve independently authorized grants. Stage 1 checks partner assignments when a partner is present; a partner is not required on every opportunity. Access rules.
  • Require Opportunity Source plus its applicable sourcing lookup at creation: Direct or BDR → Sourcing User; Marketing → Sourcing Campaign; Partner → Sourcing Partner. Show and require only the applicable lookup. A blank/--None-- source is not a partner selection and cannot pass validation.
  • Populate attribution from verified origin: Direct → originating seller User; BDR → originating BDR User; Marketing → originating Campaign; Partner → originating Partner Account. A registration may be linked for any source and does not itself prove partner origination. Partner source does not require a registration. Never use the integration user, current owner or Transacting Partner as a substitute for origin evidence.
  • Apply the same dependency and population rules to manual creation, conversion, imports and integrations. If the originating reference is missing or ambiguous, require the applicable selection or resolve the intake error before creating the opportunity; never invent an attribution. Do not overwrite a valid sourcing reference on routine edits or ownership changes. On an authorized source correction, require the newly applicable lookup, exclude inactive sourcing lookups from current attribution and preserve previous values and the change in audit history. Keep Opportunity Source attribution separate from Stage 1 Route to Market and from partner-contribution timing.
  • Require the core creation fields and preserve Opportunity Source when ownership changes.
  • An Opportunity must retain a valid User owner. Route pending assignment to a Sales Ops review list or an assigned Task; do not assign the Opportunity itself to a Salesforce queue. Notify the accountable seller when assigned, and record acceptance separately.
  • Allow controlled Budgetary quoting under the Quoting activity rules. Default the creation flow to Pre-pipeline; derive the saved sales forecast from its initial stage.
  • Snapshot creation owner/channel origin for attribution; later ownership changes do not alter partner-only origin.
  • MAP supplies MQL (Marketing Qualified Lead) to the existing Contact lifecycle. PAX.AI supplies AQL (Agentic Qualified Lead) to the Contact. These are distinct entry paths, not mandatory sequential stages. Creating an opportunity records SAL (Sales Accepted Lead) for its linked conversion Contact and the new Opportunity Conversion Lifecycle. Inbound marketing, BDR, partner/deal-registration and other non-self-qualified intake enters Stage 0 for SAL review. First valid entry into Stages 1–8 records SQL (Sales Qualified Lead) after the applicable acceptance and source-dependent CHAMPS gate, including permitted skips. A seller creating a self-qualified opportunity directly in Stage 1 may auto-accept and qualify it: record SAL and SQL events at creation, with the seller and direct-entry path; do not manufacture prior MQL/AQL events. Sales-generated CHAMPS remains required before Stage 2.
  • Use an explicitly designated originating/conversion Contact linked through Opportunity Contact Roles; reuse a supported existing primary/originating Contact relationship before adding storage. Require a resolved Contact association for creation/conversion tracking and before Stage 1; do not update every buying-committee member or infer the person from an account alone. The account is the linked Contact account in the same deal context; flag conflicting associations for review. Extend the existing Contact lifecycle and Account Lifecycle Stage (ALS) mappings rather than creating parallel Contact/Account lifecycle fields. Show per-contact conversion and related-opportunity evidence on the Account; preserve its current lifecycle semantics and customer states until the rollup mapping is approved. One new Opportunity Conversion Lifecycle field shows SAL/SQL for this opportunity; retain it as the last conversion milestone after discovery, separate from sales Stage.
  • PAX.AI is the qualification channel, not a newly approved Opportunity Source choice. Its integration must provide the underlying source and applicable sourcing lookup under the current source-attribution rules; resolve that mapping before automated opportunity creation. Do not invent a campaign, sourcing user or partner, and do not classify an AI-origin record as seller-self-qualified simply because an integration created it.
  • Future dependency — Marketing MAP program: today Sourcing Campaign remains the existing Pardot campaign lookup (current system described by Marketing). At the MAP cutover it will become Marketing Source, mapped from the marketing automation platform source. This is a future field/integration change, not a rename or replacement in the current release. Confirm source IDs, data model, historical campaign crosswalk and multi-touch reporting with Marketing before migration; preserve current attribution and historical campaign references. Opportunity Source still provides the high-level source classification.
  • For forward moves through numbered sales stages, enforce all prerequisite stage gates crossed, including assignment/acceptance, discovery, technical fit/POC, Technical Win, procurement and OM booking authority. Apply the same checks to UI, imports, integrations and automation; a stage skip cannot bypass a gate. New sales opportunities start at 0, or at 1 only for the documented assigned seller-self-qualified path. Loss uses its own loss-entry rules and does not require completing earlier sales gates. Preserve the documented Stage 6 → 5 quote-correction path and booked-record locks.
  • Retain the existing Duplicate, Transacted-Internal Order and Transacted-Reference Opty administrative outcomes and their current handling. They are not additional sales stages or Closed lost outcomes. No retirement or historical migration of those values is requested by this design.
  • Implementation prerequisite — RevOps must confirm the existing Bypass Validations permissions, affected rules and audit evidence. Do not grant new bypass access or treat that field as blanket authority to ignore the new gates. Preserve approved migration/historical-record exceptions; record authorized use. Resolve the rule-by-rule scope before enabling the new validations.
  • Capture a resolved originating Contact at creation for conversion tracking; maintain buying roles through discovery and require at least one assigned Opportunity Contact Role before Stage 2. These are separate checks. Reuse a verified existing originating/primary Contact relationship or a designated Contact Role; confirm the exact relationship and creation transaction before configuration. Primary Contact remains optional as a committee designation unless it is the approved conversion-link mapping. Do not require every buying-committee member at creation.
  • Manage Forecast Category (Sales) automatically from the opportunity sales stage on creation and each permitted stage change, including UI, import and integration paths. Creation / Stage 0 → Pre-pipeline; Stages 1–2 → Pipeline; Stages 3–4 → Upside; Stages 5–6 → Commit; Stages 7–8 → Won; Closed lost → Loss. Creation is a capture step, not another Stage value: default the creation flow to Pre-pipeline, then derive the saved category from the actual initial stage. Seller-self-qualified creation directly in Stage 1 saves Pipeline immediately. An allowed return to Stage 0 restores Pre-pipeline; retain booked-record locks and existing administrative-outcome handling. Prevent or correct manual/integration category values that conflict with the stage; users do not maintain this field separately.
0

Stage specification

Validate

Assign the seller, review the SAL and qualify inbound marketing, PAX, BDR and partner/deal-registration handoffs before SQL.

Owner · Marketing + Partners / BDRs · seller assignment · Forecast: Pre-pipeline

Advance to 1 · Discovery

The assigned AE has accepted the handoff and all five inbound CHAMPS answers plus the originating Contact are complete. Creating a SAL is not acceptance. Seller-self-qualified direct Stage 1 creation follows the separate documented path.

Automations and validations

  • On successful first entry, stamp Stage 0 Date; on first entry to Stages 1–8 also stamp Pipeline Date and Pipeline Entry Amount once. Use Update Stage to complete missing evidence before progressing. Skipped stages stay blank; re-entry never resets first-entry values. Access rules.
  • Exclude Stage 0 from qualified pipeline totals and pipeline-generation credit. Pipeline Date records the first valid entry into any numbered Stage 1–8, including allowed seller-qualified creation directly in Stage 1. Capture Pipeline Entry Amount and currency context on that same event. Stamp once; preserve the timestamp and entry amount on regression, re-entry, retries and later Amount/quote changes. A permitted 0 → 4 move stamps Pipeline Date equal to Stage 4 Date; skipped Stage 1 Date remains blank. Closed lost and administrative statuses do not trigger pipeline entry. Reuse the previously proposed first-entry snapshot design; do not create a second credit mechanism. Current Amount and Created Date remain separate. Deduplicate by Opportunity ID and event. Existing creation paths, all crossed gates and booked-record locks still apply. Backfill only from trustworthy history; never substitute current Amount for missing historical entry value. Allow Budgetary CPQ quotes in Stages 0–1 with a linked Account and Opportunity, assigned seller, customer need, indicative scope/configuration, estimate assumptions and existing commercial authority. Capture Route to Market before early release; Direct requires the route notification. Early pricing does not confer SAL acceptance, SQL, Technical Win or stage advancement. Stages 2–5 retain quoting access; Best and Final requires Technical Win, selected configuration and applicable commercial approvals before customer release. Preserve Stage 6 → 5 correction and booked-record locks. Confirm the existing approval matrix; no new numeric approval threshold is specified. Access rules.
  • Route by the agreed assignment rules and surface unassigned records for review.
  • All non-self-qualified inbound intake—including Marketing, partner/deal registration, BDR-origin and PAX.AI—must complete all five existing CHAMPS fields before Stage 1. Determine this from preserved intake origin and qualification path; reassignment to an AE does not remove the gate. Seller-self-qualified opportunities may start in Stage 1 and must complete CHAMPS before Stage 2. Keep CHAMPS - Challenge separate from Identify Pain.
  • Preserve original source and attribution evidence. Allow controlled Budgetary quotes and keep Forecast Category (Sales) at Pre-pipeline.
  • Record the assigned AE acceptance decision with actor and time separately from SAL creation and SQL qualification. RevOps acting for the AE must retain evidence of that acceptance. The first qualified entry to Stages 1–8 checks acceptance, assignment, CHAMPS and every crossed gate before recording SQL, including permitted skips.
  • Carry forward the validated Opportunity Source and applicable Sourcing User, Campaign or Partner from creation; assignment changes must not overwrite origin attribution.
  • Creating the opportunity records the SAL lifecycle milestone, not the AE acceptance decision. Use existing SAL Status for Pending review, Accepted and Rejected business meanings, mapped to its actual current choices before configuration. SAL AE identifies the accountable seller. For inbound records, prevent entry to Stages 1–8 while pending or rejected; acceptance alone does not bypass CHAMPS or assignment. For Marketing-source SALs, require an acceptance date when accepted; require rejection date and explanatory notes when rejected. Notes remain available throughout review. Stamp actor and decision time through the acceptance/rejection action rather than manual date entry. Retain submissions, start and completion history separately: do not equate SAL Start Date with acceptance or SAL Completion Date with rejection without verifying their definitions. A rejected SAL does not become SQL or automatically become a Closed lost opportunity; retain its rejection disposition for conversion reporting.
  • On first valid entry from Stage 0 into Stages 1–8, after acceptance, assignment, CHAMPS and all crossed gates pass, stamp SQL for this Opportunity and linked Contact episode; sync the approved Account view. Retain source, qualification channel (MAP / PAX.AI / seller / partner / BDR), event timestamp, actor/system, Contact, Account and Opportunity relationship in conversion history. Reuse suitable existing lifecycle history/event storage, extending only missing data. Record MQL or AQL at Contact qualification; SAL at opportunity creation; accepted/rejected decision separately; SQL on first qualified entry to Stages 1–8, including permitted skips. Preserve every episode for multiple opportunities or requalification and deduplicate retries with the source event/episode ID. Late MQL/AQL messages, stage regression and a new Stage 0 opportunity must not downgrade a Contact or Account already further through its lifecycle. Keep opportunity-level events so separate deals remain reportable. Report MQL→SAL→SQL and AQL→SAL→SQL as separate cohorts, including rejected/pending SALs and elapsed time. Seller-direct SQLs remain a separate cohort; never inflate inbound conversion by fabricating MQL/AQL history.
  • Block inbound entry to Stages 1–8 until the assigned AE has accepted and all applicable qualification and crossed gates pass; record SQL at the successful transition, including permitted skips.
  • For forward moves through numbered sales stages, enforce all prerequisite stage gates crossed, including assignment/acceptance, discovery, technical fit/POC, Technical Win, procurement and OM booking authority. Apply the same checks to UI, imports, integrations and automation; a stage skip cannot bypass a gate. New sales opportunities start at 0, or at 1 only for the documented assigned seller-self-qualified path. Loss uses its own loss-entry rules and does not require completing earlier sales gates. Preserve the documented Stage 6 → 5 quote-correction path and booked-record locks.
  • Automatically maintain Forecast Category (Sales) = Pre-pipeline on entry and each permitted stage change. Apply through UI, imports and integrations; prevent or correct a value that conflicts with the stage. Keep this separate from standard Salesforce forecast fields.

Stage specification

Discovery

Record SQL at qualified pipeline entry and complete business-fit discovery before Stage 2. Controlled Budgetary quoting is available here.

Owner · Sales + Sales Engineering · Forecast: Pipeline

Advance to 2 · Solution design

Complete discovery and all five CHAMPS answers, confirm AE and Primary SE, record Route to Market and any indirect subtype, and assign at least one Opportunity Contact Role. SQL is recorded at qualified pipeline entry, including valid skips; this exit does not duplicate it.

Add each active user to the opportunity team only when the partner AND approved geography/territory coverage match. Send missing or mismatched coverage to Partner Ops; do not silently broaden access. Deduplicate membership across associations, and reconcile owner changes, coverage transfers, deactivation and expiry using the recorded grant reason. Remove only access derived from the changed assignment; preserve independently authorized grants. Stage 1 checks partner assignments when a partner is present; a partner is not required on every opportunity. On Direct route selection or change, notify the sales manager and relevant partner coverage owner before customer-facing pricing. Partner overlays follow scoped collaboration rights. Access rules.

  • Show and require Indirect Route Type only when Route to Market = Indirect; allow Direct without a second selection.
  • When changed to Direct, make the indirect type inactive for attribution and hide it; preserve any historical selection in audit history.
  • Validate at least one Contact Role and reconcile the Primary SE assignment with the opportunity team.
  • Allow controlled Budgetary quotes in Stage 1; forecast remains Pipeline.
  • Track separate Next Step Reviewed At and SE Next Step Reviewed At system timestamps, reusing existing history where it supports weekly reporting. An AE review action confirms Next Step and expected Close Date at least weekly, even when their text/date is unchanged; it stamps AE reviewer/time without changing the booked date. SE reviews its technical next action separately. Report open Stage 1–6 records not reviewed for seven days; unrelated edits do not reset freshness. Confirm the timestamp storage mapping before implementation.
  • An active Associated accounts record linked to the canonical NVIDIA Account makes NVIDIA relevant. On that association activation and on stage advancement, require the existing Opportunity Nvidia Engagement and its separately verified NVIDIA Engagement Status. After an association becomes inactive, preserve history and stop requiring new engagement input. Status choices and the Opportunity API mapping must be verified before enabling this rule; do not substitute the association Active/Inactive status.
  • Qualified pipeline entry records SQL, including a permitted skip past Discovery; a skipped Stage 1 Date remains blank. Direct seller-created Stage 1 opportunities record auto-accepted SAL and SQL events at creation; inbound opportunities require the Stage 0 acceptance and CHAMPS gate. Preserve the existing sales-generated CHAMPS requirement before Stage 2. Retain source, qualification channel (MAP / PAX.AI / seller / partner / BDR), event timestamp, actor/system, Contact, Account and Opportunity relationship in conversion history. Reuse suitable existing lifecycle history/event storage, extending only missing data. Record MQL or AQL at Contact qualification; SAL at opportunity creation; accepted/rejected decision separately; SQL on first qualified entry to Stages 1–8, including permitted skips. Preserve every episode for multiple opportunities or requalification and deduplicate retries with the source event/episode ID. Late MQL/AQL messages, stage regression and a new Stage 0 opportunity must not downgrade a Contact or Account already further through its lifecycle. Keep opportunity-level events so separate deals remain reportable. Report MQL→SAL→SQL and AQL→SAL→SQL as separate cohorts, including rejected/pending SALs and elapsed time. Seller-direct SQLs remain a separate cohort; never inflate inbound conversion by fabricating MQL/AQL history.
  • For forward moves through numbered sales stages, enforce all prerequisite stage gates crossed, including assignment/acceptance, discovery, technical fit/POC, Technical Win, procurement and OM booking authority. Apply the same checks to UI, imports, integrations and automation; a stage skip cannot bypass a gate. New sales opportunities start at 0, or at 1 only for the documented assigned seller-self-qualified path. Loss uses its own loss-entry rules and does not require completing earlier sales gates. Preserve the documented Stage 6 → 5 quote-correction path and booked-record locks.
  • Automatically maintain Forecast Category (Sales) = Pipeline on entry and each permitted stage change. Apply through UI, imports and integrations; prevent or correct a value that conflicts with the stage. Keep this separate from standard Salesforce forecast fields.

A completed POC or valid configuration alone is not a win. Blank, In Progress and Technical Loss cannot pass the Stage 3 → 4 gate or bypass it by skipping to Commit. New sales opportunities start at 0, or at 1 only for the documented assigned seller-self-qualified path. Loss uses its own loss-entry rules and does not require completing earlier sales gates. Preserve the documented Stage 6 → 5 quote-correction path and booked-record locks.
  • Automatically maintain Forecast Category (Sales) = Upside on entry and each permitted stage change. Apply through UI, imports and integrations; prevent or correct a value that conflicts with the stage. Keep this separate from standard Salesforce forecast fields.

  • Transacting Partner

Automations and validations

  • On successful first entry, stamp Stage 5 Date; on first entry to Stages 1–8 also stamp Pipeline Date and Pipeline Entry Amount once. Use Update Stage to complete missing evidence before progressing. Skipped stages stay blank; re-entry never resets first-entry values. Access rules.
  • Sales maintains procurement evidence; Finance and delegated commercial approvers validate their own controls. Access alone never grants approval authority. Access rules.
  • Require all Stage 5 fields and valid IPG disposition before 5 → 6.
  • Validate PO Secured AND a real attached PO; a checkbox alone cannot pass the gate.
  • Reconcile pre-order delivery requests into existing Order fields during handoff. Keep estimates, requested delivery and actual events distinct.
  • Set forecast to Commit. Quoting remains available in Stage 5 and becomes unavailable on entering Stage 6.
  • Entry to Won decision (6) additionally requires the Win/Loss win fields and applicable interview / amount-dependent inputs; do not defer those to the 6 → 7 booking gate.
  • Allow linked product-feature Cases to be created in Stages 2–5 only; require title, description and Opportunity.
  • Review lead time and logistics; retain the Stage 4 installation and Stage 5 shipping/delivery field gates. Reconcile with OM at booking.
  • Identify the actual PO File using existing document-type metadata or an explicit PO File reference; Sales identifies it and OM verifies the content. A random attachment does not satisfy the PO requirement.
  • If OM finds a quote/product/price discrepancy in Stage 6, record the reason and return the opportunity to Stage 5 for Sales/SE correction in CPQ. Revalidate the procurement and Won decision entry gates before returning to 6; do not resend the same win notification. Quote creation/revision is allowed in Stages 2–5, with controlled Budgetary-only access in Stages 0–1; it remains blocked at Stage 6 and after booking. After booking, use the existing change-order/amendment process linked to the booked order; do not reopen the booked opportunity or bypass its quote and Close Date locks. Any amendment needing a new sales quote uses a separately linked opportunity in Stages 2–5.
  • Track separate Next Step Reviewed At and SE Next Step Reviewed At system timestamps, reusing existing history where it supports weekly reporting. An AE review action confirms Next Step and expected Close Date at least weekly, even when their text/date is unchanged; it stamps AE reviewer/time without changing the booked date. SE reviews its technical next action separately. Report open Stage 1–6 records not reviewed for seven days; unrelated edits do not reset freshness. Confirm the timestamp storage mapping before implementation.
  • An active Associated accounts record linked to the canonical NVIDIA Account makes NVIDIA relevant. On that association activation and on stage advancement, require the existing Opportunity Nvidia Engagement and its separately verified NVIDIA Engagement Status. After an association becomes inactive, preserve history and stop requiring new engagement input. Status choices and the Opportunity API mapping must be verified before enabling this rule; do not substitute the association Active/Inactive status.
  • CPQ quote inputs: required Quote Purpose (Budgetary / Best and Final); required Timing Urgency defaulting to Standard; Urgent requires Urgency Reason. Competitor Pricing is optional on the quote. Validate through the Quoting activity; these are not additional Opportunity fields.
  • Applicability to confirm — OM and GSS must define the logistics dates, deployment-Case requirement and Stage 8 completion evidence for renewals, software/services, Internal Transfer, Non-Revenue and No Shipping required orders. The physical-delivery path applies where shipping/deployment is in scope. Do not invent delivery dates, create a dummy Case or treat shipment as receipt to satisfy it. Confirm the applicable alternative before enabling universal logistics/Case validation; no new exception status or field is approved here.
  • For forward moves through numbered sales stages, enforce all prerequisite stage gates crossed, including assignment/acceptance, discovery, technical fit/POC, Technical Win, procurement and OM booking authority. Apply the same checks to UI, imports, integrations and automation; a stage skip cannot bypass a gate. New sales opportunities start at 0, or at 1 only for the documented assigned seller-self-qualified path. Loss uses its own loss-entry rules and does not require completing earlier sales gates. Preserve the documented Stage 6 → 5 quote-correction path and booked-record locks.
  • Automatically maintain Forecast Category (Sales) = Commit on entry and each permitted stage change. Apply through UI, imports and integrations; prevent or correct a value that conflicts with the stage. Keep this separate from standard Salesforce forecast fields.

Existing service-case reference shown in System Info and Closed Won; candidate to carry the deployment case.RelabelPreviously: PS CaseInitiate at Stage 6 · carry through Stage 8Create or link the coordinating PS deployment Case at Won decision (6), using the existing Opportunity PS Case and Order Case(WO) relationships after confirming their targets.
Full rule

GSS starts deployment planning with Sales/SE and OM in Stage 6. Before booking at 7, identify the GSS case owner and document shipping, on-site receiving and installation/deployment plans, dates, responsibilities and dependencies on the Case and linked Order. Avoid duplicate cases on stage re-entry; associate later Orders with the same coordinating case and retain subordinate workstream cases where needed. Reuse the existing Opportunity PS Case link; keep deployment and value detail on the Case. Applicability to confirm — OM and GSS must define the logistics dates, deployment-Case requirement and Stage 8 completion evidence for renewals, software/services, Internal Transfer, Non-Revenue and No Shipping required orders. The physical-delivery path applies where shipping/deployment is in scope. Do not invent delivery dates, create a dummy Case or treat shipment as receipt to satisfy it. Confirm the applicable alternative before enabling universal logistics/Case validation; no new exception status or field is approved here.

Ongoing activities

  • Multi-partner attribution
  • Lead time and logistics
  • Partner contribution
  • Win wire
  • Confirm order
  • Deployment case

Automations and validations

  • Protect booked Close Date, Amount, Products and attribution from routine edits while permitting the authorized 7 → 8 delivery transition, verified invoice/reference synchronization and approved booked-correction workflow. Do not implement a whole-record lock that blocks required post-booking updates. Case updates never rewrite booked sales facts. Access rules.
  • On first valid pipeline entry, stamp Pipeline Date and Pipeline Entry Amount once. Preserve prior snapshots and enforce all prerequisite gates and existing booking/delivery authority. Access rules.
  • Only authorized Order Management confirms booking. Finance maintains authorized order/invoice facts; GSS maintains Case delivery and deployment evidence. Protect booked opportunity facts. Access rules.
  • OM controls booking confirmation and locks Close Date at Stage 7; GSS coordinates the deployment Case.
  • Order Management and GSS coordinate shipping, receiving and deployment through Stages 7–8. Stage 7 is the confirmed/booked PO with locked booked facts and Close Date; Stage 8 Invoice is reached when the complete in-scope order has been delivered on site. Shipment alone does not prove receipt. OM verifies receipt with GSS against every non-cancelled booked item; partial deliveries remain in 7 and approved scope changes are recorded on the Order. Keep promised/scheduled ship dates, actual shipping, receipt and planned/actual deployment distinct on the Order/Case. Record and reconcile invoice evidence on the existing Order and Opportunity references; financial invoice date remains distinct from on-site delivery. Do not create a new Opportunity deployment-status or completion-date field.
  • Create or link the coordinating PS deployment Case at Won decision (6), using the existing Opportunity PS Case and Order Case(WO) relationships after confirming their targets. GSS starts deployment planning with Sales/SE and OM in Stage 6. Before booking at 7, identify the GSS case owner and document shipping, on-site receiving and installation/deployment plans, dates, responsibilities and dependencies on the Case and linked Order. Avoid duplicate cases on stage re-entry; associate later Orders with the same coordinating case and retain subordinate workstream cases where needed.
  • Capture the owner/channel classification at creation. An opportunity created and owned solely by the partner/channel is Partner-only; later ownership transfers do not rewrite that origin. Otherwise, the earliest qualifying partner association or approved deal-registration event before Stage 3 is Partner-Led; the first event in Stages 3–6 is Joint. Evaluate attribution immediately before entry to Closed won (7) and freeze automated credit at booking. Associations added at Stage 7 or later remain visible but do not change booked credit. A management override may correct reporting with an audited reason; blank override uses automated Partner Contribution. Before migration the qualifying association is Primary Partner; after migration it is the first partner in Associated accounts. End Customer-only associations do not trigger partner credit. Retain event date, event stage and origin evidence; stage regression, partner removal and routine ownership changes never reset that evidence. No qualifying event retains Sales only / existing no-partner disposition.
  • Quoting remains disabled; use the agreed amendment/change-order path for booked corrections.
  • Applicability to confirm — OM and GSS must define the logistics dates, deployment-Case requirement and Stage 8 completion evidence for renewals, software/services, Internal Transfer, Non-Revenue and No Shipping required orders. The physical-delivery path applies where shipping/deployment is in scope. Do not invent delivery dates, create a dummy Case or treat shipment as receipt to satisfy it. Confirm the applicable alternative before enabling universal logistics/Case validation; no new exception status or field is approved here.
  • Verified complete on-site delivery controls entry to Stage 8; pending financial invoicing does not block that transition. Invoice Reference and the related Order Invoice Date are required when the invoice is issued, not before delivery-led completion. OM continues invoice reconciliation on the Order after Stage 8; retain all related invoice references and dates. Do not enter placeholder invoice values or use financial Invoice Date as the delivery/time-to-value milestone.
  • For forward moves through numbered sales stages, enforce all prerequisite stage gates crossed, including assignment/acceptance, discovery, technical fit/POC, Technical Win, procurement and OM booking authority. Apply the same checks to UI, imports, integrations and automation; a stage skip cannot bypass a gate. New sales opportunities start at 0, or at 1 only for the documented assigned seller-self-qualified path. Loss uses its own loss-entry rules and does not require completing earlier sales gates. Preserve the documented Stage 6 → 5 quote-correction path and booked-record locks.
  • Automatically maintain Forecast Category (Sales) = Won on entry and each permitted stage change. Apply through UI, imports and integrations; prevent or correct a value that conflicts with the stage. Keep this separate from standard Salesforce forecast fields.
8

Stage specification

Invoice

On-site delivery completes the Opportunity lifecycle. Retain Invoice and booked results while GSS continues deployment and value realization on the Case.

Owner · Order Management + GSS · Forecast: Won

Final Opportunity stage

Retain the Invoice label and the agreed delivery-led completion rule. The deal stays closed/won. Invoice reconciliation continues on the Order; deployment and value assessment continue on the Case without reopening the deal.

On the Case, calculate Time to Value only when Value Achieved? = Yes and Value Outcome Confirmed Date is on/after that start; pending/No stays blank. A missing start is an integration exception, not a reason to reopen or hold the Opportunity.
  • Deliver the Deal continues on the PS Case, not as numbered Opportunity stages. GSS manages delivery/setup, the deployment checklist and completion evidence using existing Case fields and workstream relationships; confirm the exact status/date mappings before configuration. The coordinating Case covers all in-scope workstreams; closing one case alone does not prove full deployment. Track the sales-defined value plan, target timing, achieved Yes/No, assessment date and supporting context on the Case. Pending outcome stays blank; No requires the gap and agreed disposition. GSS uses results to inform renewals and upsell through separately linked sales opportunities. The original opportunity ends at Stage 8 and is never held open, advanced or reopened to wait for deployment or value realization.

LOST

Stage specification

Closed lost

Record the loss, conduct a lost deal review and capture learning and follow-up.

Owner · Sales + Sales Engineering · Forecast: Loss

Record the loss decision

Require reason, explanation, actual loss date and next-step recommendation. Competitor capture is optional for the three approved noncompetitive reasons; selecting Other still requires detail. Interview eligibility is optional only for Stage 0 misqualification. Loss uses its own gates, not every earlier sales-stage gate.

Lost deal review

AE and Sales Manager document the decision, loss reason, competitive context, operational / technical / business gaps, lessons and follow-up. Qualifying losses require a Deal Team Loss Report within two business days. Evaluate thresholds using the stage immediately before Closed lost.

Notification recipients for New / Existing Business: AE, Sales Manager, KD, Gary Kortye, Colin, Sales VP, Omer and Guido. Renewals add Kevin Hicks. Include opportunity age, partner context and the loss fields. Resolve recipients to current users or maintained groups during implementation.

CI selects interviews weekly; the AE provides a warm introduction within two business days if requested. Reports and interview summaries remain restricted; never post a loss report to #win-wire. The interview does not delay the report.

Win/Loss rules for this design: ≥$1M requires Resources Engaged; >$1M triggers Win wire; >$2M adds the full Win Report. Capture at entry to Won decision (6), distinct from booking at 7. Include Renewals in interview capture, keep Competitor Lost To loss-only, capture competition in Stage 2 and include Relationship once in Won Reason.

Ongoing activities

  • Lost deal review and follow-up

Automations and validations

  • Sales records the loss decision and evidence; Sales Ops / RevOps correct only within delegated authority with history retained. Access rules.
  • Require Loss Reason, Loss Reason Details and Lost — Next Step Recommendation. Opportunity Misqualified is allowed only from prior Stage 0. Competitor Lost To is optional when Loss Reason is Opportunity Misqualified, Project Cancelled / Budget Lost or No Decision / Went Dark; it remains required for other loss reasons. Preserve any supplied competitor values and do not infer a winner or default Not lost to competitor. Competitor Detail (if Other) is required only when Other is explicitly selected in Competitors or Competitor Lost To; retain that rule even for an exempt loss reason. For New Business, Existing Business and Renewal, interview eligibility remains required at won decision and loss, except Opportunity Misqualified from prior Stage 0, where it is optional. Cancelled/budget-lost and went-dark losses still require eligibility. If eligibility is blank under the Stage 0 exemption, neither interview Contact nor cannot-approach reason is required. If Yes is supplied, require Contact; if No is supplied, require WL Reason Cannot Approach. Preserve existing values and never validate the inactive branch. Notify on loss from prior Stage ≥3 with amount >$1M, or prior Stage ≥2 with amount >$5M; retain prior stage for evaluation. Set forecast to Loss and disable quoting. Historical closed records are not retro-validated; an unrealized post-sales outcome does not turn a booked sale into a loss.
  • A qualifying Deal Team Loss Report is due within two business days; keep it restricted and never post it to #win-wire.
  • At Closed lost, the AE confirms the existing Close Date reflects the actual loss decision date. Retain date-change history. Weekly open-pipeline review requirements stop on loss; do not change previously closed records retroactively or replace a booked Close Date with a post-sales outcome date.

Across the lifecycle · Customer-facing teams

Customer meetings and follow-up Tasks

Process

  1. Create one meeting Task: choose the existing meeting Type, set a specific Subject, owner and Due Date, link the customer and deal, and put the meeting objective in Comments. Keep the meeting time and invitation in the existing calendar process.
  2. Update the same Task when the meeting moves. Keep it open until it occurs; record cancellations and no-shows distinctly using the validated Status / Activity Disposition mapping.
  3. After the meeting, record the outcome, decisions and agreed actions in Comments, then complete the held-meeting Task. Create separate follow-up Tasks with owners and due dates, and update the opportunity’s Next Step or SE Next Step when the agreed next action changes.
  4. Synergy provides a voice interface for creating meeting follow-up Tasks. Review the suggested owner, date, customer/deal link and action before saving. Voice-created Tasks follow the same permissions, validation and duplicate controls as manually created Tasks.

Connected fields

Inspected in Salesforce’s New Task: Default form on 29 September 2026 using Computer Use. Field labels and the listed Type/Status values were observed; API names, storage limits, record-type permissions and dependency mappings still require configuration verification. No Task was saved.

Additional fields observed

Retain these mappings; they are not extra meeting-entry requirements.

Automations and permissions

Use a meeting-specific Task layout/action showing only the relevant fields. Validate meeting Type, Subject, owner, date and customer/deal link; completion of a held meeting requires outcome notes. Enforce edit rights for the Task owner and authorized deal collaborators, with Sales Ops / RevOps correction rights. Do not grant access to a private opportunity through a Task. Preserve integration metadata and deduplicate retries using the source activity ID where available. Report scheduled, held, cancelled/no-show and overdue follow-up separately; meetings do not advance sales stages automatically.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.

Outstanding: calendar sync. Task creation does not send or synchronize calendar invitations. Calendar ownership, invite/update/cancellation behavior and duplicate matching remain to be designed.

All ongoing activities ↑

Automations and permissions

Confirm Access Activities, Edit Tasks, Activity sharing and both Name (Who) / Related To (What) access for meeting Tasks. Opportunity team membership alone does not guarantee the ability to edit someone else’s Task. Use assigned follow-up Tasks or an explicitly authorized correction action where native access is insufficient; do not widen commercial record access to fix a Task edit.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.

Partner Technical LeadPartner Account · User lookupProposed inputs: Active internal userNot applicable — new proposed Account field.New fieldPartner Ops maintains with Sales Engineering; drives technical team assignment.
Partner Admin ContactPartner Account · Contact lookupProposed inputs: Contact associated with that partner accountNot applicable — new proposed Account field.New fieldPartner Ops maintains the partner’s administrative point of contact. This does not grant portal, approval or CRM administration rights.

Automations and permissions

On creation or activation of a partner association, resolve the partner Account’s Primary DDN Partner Account Manager and Partner Technical Lead. Add each active user to the opportunity team only when the partner AND approved geography/territory coverage match. Send missing or mismatched coverage to Partner Ops; do not silently broaden access. Deduplicate membership across associations, and reconcile owner changes, coverage transfers, deactivation and expiry using the recorded grant reason. Remove only access derived from the changed assignment; preserve independently authorized grants. Stage 1 checks partner assignments when a partner is present; a partner is not required on every opportunity. Partner overlays receive access only for their assigned partner and geo/territory coverage. Broader direct-pipeline visibility needs an explicit entitlement. Team membership does not grant forecast, Amount, commercial approval, registration approval or validation-bypass rights. Recalculate derived access when coverage changes and preserve audit history.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.

All ongoing activities ↑

Stages 0–1 Budgetary / 2–5 broader quoting · Sales + Sales Engineering

Quoting

Process

Use CPQ to add products and develop the quote. The selected Primary Quote drives Opportunity Amount and adds/synchronizes Opportunity Products. Select it by Stage 4; use controlled Budgetary quotes in Stages 0–1 and regular quote creation/revision in Stages 2–5.

New quote fields

Add these fields to the CPQ quote creation and editing forms. Quote Purpose is separate from the existing CPQ Type field.

Field / typeInputsRequired / defaultPurpose / help text
Quote PurposePicklistNew fieldBudgetary / Best and FinalRequired; no default.Identify whether the quote is budgetary or best and final.
Timing UrgencyPicklistNew fieldStandard / UrgentRequired; default Standard on new quotes.Flag urgency only when needed; selecting Urgent makes Urgency Reason required.
Urgency ReasonText areaNew fieldFree text; no fixed dropdown choices.Required only when Timing Urgency = Urgent.Explain why the quote is urgent. Whitespace-only input does not satisfy the requirement. Proposed text limit to be set during configuration.
Competitor PricingText areaNew fieldFree text; no fixed dropdown choices.Optional.Help text: Do we have visibility into competitor pricing? Capture available pricing context; leave blank when unknown. Proposed text limit to be set during configuration.

Quote validations

On CPQ quote creation and editable quote saves, require Quote Purpose = Budgetary or Best and Final and Timing Urgency = Standard or Urgent. Default Timing Urgency to Standard on new quotes; Quote Purpose has no default and must be selected. If Timing Urgency = Urgent, show and require a nonblank Urgency Reason; Standard does not require a reason. Competitor Pricing is optional. Apply the validation across supported UI and integration save paths; apply controlled Budgetary access in Stages 0–1 and regular quoting in Stages 2–5, with Best and Final subject to technical and commercial gates.

Retain existing Primary Quote synchronization to Opportunity Amount and Products. These additions belong on the Quote, not the Opportunity. Historical locked quotes do not require a bulk backfill; apply the rules when creating or editing an editable quote.

Creating a CPQ quote

The supplied New Quote screenshot shows Account and Opportunity context, Name, Legal Entity, Bill To Account, Ship To Account, Bill To Contact, Ship To Contact, Primary Contact and Expires On. Legal Entity is the only visible required marker in that creation dialog. Name has a 128-character limit in the inspected edit form. The full edit form additionally marks DDN Terms & Conditions, Bill To Account, Ship To Account and Install Countries required; validate those downstream requirements separately from the create dialog.

Creation gate: linked Opportunity must be in Stages 2–5, or Stages 0–1 for controlled Budgetary quoting only. Populate the quote by adding products, then use the Primary Quote to update Opportunity Amount and Products. No quote was created or saved during this review.

Quote-line columns: Line Name, Product, Config Part, List Unit Price, Customer Unit Price, Total Discount (%), Quantity, Net Total, Covered Asset and Product Status. Quote-line inputs were not edited; keep product/pricing detail on CPQ lines.

Related tabs inspected: Lines & Groups (product / quote-line information, groups, pricing guidance and additional documents); Sales Orders; Files & Attachments (quote documents, notes and attachments); Approvals & History (approvals, archived approvals, field history and activities). These remain related records, not new Opportunity fields.

Operating rules

Create-form requirements and the captured quote inventory are below. Quote calculation/product synchronization needs regression validation; do not replace it with manual Opportunity amount or product entry. If OM finds a quote/product/price discrepancy in Stage 6, record the reason and return the opportunity to Stage 5 for Sales/SE correction in CPQ. Revalidate the procurement and Won decision entry gates before returning to 6; do not resend the same win notification. Quote creation/revision is allowed in Stages 2–5, with controlled Budgetary-only access in Stages 0–1; it remains blocked at Stage 6 and after booking. After booking, use the existing change-order/amendment process linked to the booked order; do not reopen the booked opportunity or bypass its quote and Close Date locks. Any amendment needing a new sales quote uses a separately linked opportunity in Stages 2–5. On CPQ quote creation and editable quote saves, require Quote Purpose = Budgetary or Best and Final and Timing Urgency = Standard or Urgent. Default Timing Urgency to Standard on new quotes; Quote Purpose has no default and must be selected. If Timing Urgency = Urgent, show and require a nonblank Urgency Reason; Standard does not require a reason. Competitor Pricing is optional. Apply the validation across supported UI and integration save paths; apply controlled Budgetary access in Stages 0–1 and regular quoting in Stages 2–5, with Best and Final subject to technical and commercial gates.

All ongoing activities ↑

Automations and permissions

Allow Budgetary CPQ quotes in Stages 0–1 with a linked Account and Opportunity, assigned seller, customer need, indicative scope/configuration, estimate assumptions and existing commercial authority. Capture Route to Market before early release; Direct requires the route notification. Early pricing does not confer SAL acceptance, SQL, Technical Win or stage advancement. Stages 2–5 retain quoting access; Best and Final requires Technical Win, selected configuration and applicable commercial approvals before customer release. Preserve Stage 6 → 5 correction and booked-record locks. Confirm the existing approval matrix; no new numeric approval threshold is specified. On Direct route selection or change, notify the sales manager and relevant partner coverage owner before customer-facing pricing. Record the notification against the opportunity and resolve missing coverage through Partner Ops. Capture the route during Stage 1, or earlier for a Stage 0–1 quote. This notification is not a new blanket approval gate; existing commercial approvals still apply.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.

Stages Creation–1 · Marketing + Sales / BDR + Partner teams + RevOps

Conversion tracking

Process

MAP supplies MQL (Marketing Qualified Lead) to the existing Contact lifecycle. PAX.AI supplies AQL (Agentic Qualified Lead) to the Contact. These are distinct entry paths, not mandatory sequential stages. Creating an opportunity records SAL (Sales Accepted Lead) for its linked conversion Contact and the new Opportunity Conversion Lifecycle. Inbound marketing, BDR, partner/deal-registration and other non-self-qualified intake enters Stage 0 for SAL review. First valid entry into Stages 1–8 records SQL (Sales Qualified Lead) after the applicable acceptance and source-dependent CHAMPS gate, including permitted skips. A seller creating a self-qualified opportunity directly in Stage 1 may auto-accept and qualify it: record SAL and SQL events at creation, with the seller and direct-entry path; do not manufacture prior MQL/AQL events. Sales-generated CHAMPS remains required before Stage 2.

Connected fields

Field / typeInputsCurrent purposeChangeUpdated purpose / placement
Conversion ContactRelated records

Select/link an existing Salesforce Contact; no new buying-role choice is required. Exact originating-contact relationship mapping must be confirmed.

Associates existing Contacts with the opportunity and records their buying roles.Extend trackingCapture a resolved originating Contact at creation for conversion tracking; maintain buying roles through discovery and require at least one assigned Opportunity Contact Role before Stage 2.
Full rule

These are separate checks. Reuse a verified existing originating/primary Contact relationship or a designated Contact Role; confirm the exact relationship and creation transaction before configuration. Primary Contact remains optional as a committee designation unless it is the approved conversion-link mapping. Do not require every buying-committee member at creation. Use an explicitly designated originating/conversion Contact linked through Opportunity Contact Roles; reuse a supported existing primary/originating Contact relationship before adding storage. Require a resolved Contact association for creation/conversion tracking and before Stage 1; do not update every buying-committee member or infer the person from an account alone. The account is the linked Contact account in the same deal context; flag conflicting associations for review. Extend the existing Contact lifecycle and Account Lifecycle Stage (ALS) mappings rather than creating parallel Contact/Account lifecycle fields. Show per-contact conversion and related-opportunity evidence on the Account; preserve its current lifecycle semantics and customer states until the rollup mapping is approved. One new Opportunity Conversion Lifecycle field shows SAL/SQL for this opportunity; retain it as the last conversion milestone after discovery, separate from sales Stage.

SAL StatusExisting read-only display · schema/control type unverified

Existing field confirmed in Salesforce; no editable input or choice list exposed on the inspected form. Confirm its API/type and existing automation before changing the control.

Displays the existing SAL handoff disposition.Validation / automationCreating the opportunity records the SAL lifecycle milestone, not the AE acceptance decision. Use existing SAL Status for Pending review, Accepted and Rejected business meanings, mapped to its actual current choices before configuration. SAL AE identifies the accountable seller. For inbound records, prevent entry to Stages 1–8 while pending or rejected; acceptance alone does not bypass CHAMPS or assignment. For Marketing-source SALs, require an acceptance date when accepted; require rejection date and explanatory notes when rejected. Notes remain available throughout review. Stamp actor and decision time through the acceptance/rejection action rather than manual date entry. Retain submissions, start and completion history separately: do not equate SAL Start Date with acceptance or SAL Completion Date with rejection without verifying their definitions. A rejected SAL does not become SQL or automatically become a Closed lost opportunity; retain its rejection disposition for conversion reporting.

Sourcing CampaignLookup/search control (UI captured; Campaign target to verify)

Select a related Campaign record using the existing search control; no fixed choice list. The observed 255-character control limit is a search-input limit, not an attribution text field.

Records the campaign credited with sourcing the opportunity.Validation / automationRequired for Marketing. Populate from the originating Campaign. Future dependency — Marketing MAP program: today Sourcing Campaign remains the existing Pardot campaign lookup (current system described by Marketing). At the MAP cutover it will become Marketing Source, mapped from the marketing automation platform source. This is a future field/integration change, not a rename or replacement in the current release. Confirm source IDs, data model, historical campaign crosswalk and multi-touch reporting with Marketing before migration; preserve current attribution and historical campaign references. Opportunity Source still provides the high-level source classification.

Lifecycle events

MAP → MQL or PAX.AI → AQL on Contact → opportunity created: SAL → Qualified pipeline entry: SQL.

Seller-qualified direct entry records SAL + SQL together; it does not invent an MQL/AQL event. SAL lifecycle entry and the reviewer’s acceptance decision are separate events.

Current field evidence

SAL AE, SAL Type, SAL Status, SAL Submission Date, SAL Start Date and SAL Completion Date were confirmed in Salesforce on 17 Sep 2026. They were read-only on the inspected form. Acceptance/rejection dates and notes were not exposed as separate controls; their existing mappings and limits remain to be verified.

Future MAP dependency

Future dependency — Marketing MAP program: today Sourcing Campaign remains the existing Pardot campaign lookup (current system described by Marketing). At the MAP cutover it will become Marketing Source, mapped from the marketing automation platform source. This is a future field/integration change, not a rename or replacement in the current release. Confirm source IDs, data model, historical campaign crosswalk and multi-touch reporting with Marketing before migration; preserve current attribution and historical campaign references. Opportunity Source still provides the high-level source classification.

Operating rules

Creating the opportunity records the SAL lifecycle milestone, not the AE acceptance decision. Use existing SAL Status for Pending review, Accepted and Rejected business meanings, mapped to its actual current choices before configuration. SAL AE identifies the accountable seller. For inbound records, prevent entry to Stages 1–8 while pending or rejected; acceptance alone does not bypass CHAMPS or assignment. For Marketing-source SALs, require an acceptance date when accepted; require rejection date and explanatory notes when rejected. Notes remain available throughout review. Stamp actor and decision time through the acceptance/rejection action rather than manual date entry. Retain submissions, start and completion history separately: do not equate SAL Start Date with acceptance or SAL Completion Date with rejection without verifying their definitions. A rejected SAL does not become SQL or automatically become a Closed lost opportunity; retain its rejection disposition for conversion reporting. Use an explicitly designated originating/conversion Contact linked through Opportunity Contact Roles; reuse a supported existing primary/originating Contact relationship before adding storage. Require a resolved Contact association for creation/conversion tracking and before Stage 1; do not update every buying-committee member or infer the person from an account alone. The account is the linked Contact account in the same deal context; flag conflicting associations for review. Extend the existing Contact lifecycle and Account Lifecycle Stage (ALS) mappings rather than creating parallel Contact/Account lifecycle fields. Show per-contact conversion and related-opportunity evidence on the Account; preserve its current lifecycle semantics and customer states until the rollup mapping is approved. One new Opportunity Conversion Lifecycle field shows SAL/SQL for this opportunity; retain it as the last conversion milestone after discovery, separate from sales Stage. Retain source, qualification channel (MAP / PAX.AI / seller / partner / BDR), event timestamp, actor/system, Contact, Account and Opportunity relationship in conversion history. Reuse suitable existing lifecycle history/event storage, extending only missing data. Record MQL or AQL at Contact qualification; SAL at opportunity creation; accepted/rejected decision separately; SQL on first qualified entry to Stages 1–8, including permitted skips. Preserve every episode for multiple opportunities or requalification and deduplicate retries with the source event/episode ID. Late MQL/AQL messages, stage regression and a new Stage 0 opportunity must not downgrade a Contact or Account already further through its lifecycle. Keep opportunity-level events so separate deals remain reportable. Report MQL→SAL→SQL and AQL→SAL→SQL as separate cohorts, including rejected/pending SALs and elapsed time. Seller-direct SQLs remain a separate cohort; never inflate inbound conversion by fabricating MQL/AQL history. PAX.AI is the qualification channel, not a newly approved Opportunity Source choice. Its integration must provide the underlying source and applicable sourcing lookup under the current source-attribution rules; resolve that mapping before automated opportunity creation. Do not invent a campaign, sourcing user or partner, and do not classify an AI-origin record as seller-self-qualified simply because an integration created it. Record the assigned AE acceptance decision with actor and time separately from SAL creation and SQL qualification. RevOps acting for the AE must retain evidence of that acceptance. The first qualified entry to Stages 1–8 checks acceptance, assignment, CHAMPS and every crossed gate before recording SQL, including permitted skips. All non-self-qualified inbound intake—including Marketing, partner/deal registration, BDR-origin and PAX.AI—must complete all five existing CHAMPS fields before Stage 1. Determine this from preserved intake origin and qualification path; reassignment to an AE does not remove the gate. Seller-self-qualified opportunities may start in Stage 1 and must complete CHAMPS before Stage 2. Keep CHAMPS - Challenge separate from Identify Pain. Capture a resolved originating Contact at creation for conversion tracking; maintain buying roles through discovery and require at least one assigned Opportunity Contact Role before Stage 2. These are separate checks. Reuse a verified existing originating/primary Contact relationship or a designated Contact Role; confirm the exact relationship and creation transaction before configuration. Primary Contact remains optional as a committee designation unless it is the approved conversion-link mapping. Do not require every buying-committee member at creation.

All ongoing activities ↑

Automations and permissions

Exclude Stage 0 from qualified pipeline totals and pipeline-generation credit. Pipeline Date records the first valid entry into any numbered Stage 1–8, including allowed seller-qualified creation directly in Stage 1. Capture Pipeline Entry Amount and currency context on that same event. Stamp once; preserve the timestamp and entry amount on regression, re-entry, retries and later Amount/quote changes. A permitted 0 → 4 move stamps Pipeline Date equal to Stage 4 Date; skipped Stage 1 Date remains blank. Closed lost and administrative statuses do not trigger pipeline entry. Reuse the previously proposed first-entry snapshot design; do not create a second credit mechanism. Current Amount and Created Date remain separate. Deduplicate by Opportunity ID and event. Existing creation paths, all crossed gates and booked-record locks still apply. Backfill only from trustworthy history; never substitute current Amount for missing historical entry value. RevOps owns lifecycle mappings; sellers cannot edit system event timestamps.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.

Automations and permissions

Provide Stage 0 Date through Stage 6 Date as system-maintained Date/Time fields for first actual entry to each stage. Stamp the applicable field once on a successful transition or permitted creation directly in that stage. Leave never-visited stages blank, including stages crossed by a skip; do not reset on regression or re-entry. Use the same event timestamp for the destination Stage Date and Pipeline Date when both first qualify. Retain transition history for time spent across repeated visits; first-entry differences alone are not total stage duration. Backfill only from trustworthy history and reuse a verified equivalent field instead of duplicating storage. Business users cannot edit these values; failed or cancelled transitions stamp nothing.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.

Automations and permissions

Record SQL once for the Opportunity and linked Contact episode on its first valid entry into numbered Stages 1–8 after the applicable assignment, acceptance and qualification gates pass. This includes permitted skips and seller-self-qualified direct Stage 1 creation. Use the same successful transition as Pipeline Date. Completing qualification while still in Stage 0 does not by itself record SQL. A skipped Stage 1 Date stays blank; do not invent a Discovery visit, duplicate SQL on re-entry, or fabricate MQL/AQL history.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.

Reference Partner Opportunity Influence record. Inspected fields and inputs are retained above. The display name becomes Associated accounts; the separate Partner Association relationship remains.

Operating rules

Require one primary Role On Deal and one Account Classification per Associated Account record. Keep the existing single-select role and extend its choices to Transacting, Influencer, Solution / Technical Advisor, Implementation / Services, End Customer and Other. Capture secondary contributions in Association Description / Plan; do not duplicate the Account–Opportunity association merely to add a role. Preserve existing Transacting / Influencer values. A distinct approved registration can remain linked without duplicating account credit. Capture the owner/channel classification at creation. An opportunity created and owned solely by the partner/channel is Partner-only; later ownership transfers do not rewrite that origin. Otherwise, the earliest qualifying partner association or approved deal-registration event before Stage 3 is Partner-Led; the first event in Stages 3–6 is Joint. Evaluate attribution immediately before entry to Closed won (7) and freeze automated credit at booking. Associations added at Stage 7 or later remain visible but do not change booked credit. A management override may correct reporting with an audited reason; blank override uses automated Partner Contribution. Before migration the qualifying association is Primary Partner; after migration it is the first partner in Associated accounts. End Customer-only associations do not trigger partner credit. Retain event date, event stage and origin evidence; stage regression, partner removal and routine ownership changes never reset that evidence. No qualifying event retains Sales only / existing no-partner disposition.

All ongoing activities ↑

Automations and permissions

On creation or activation of a partner association, resolve the partner Account’s Primary DDN Partner Account Manager and Partner Technical Lead. Add each active user to the opportunity team only when the partner AND approved geography/territory coverage match. Send missing or mismatched coverage to Partner Ops; do not silently broaden access. Deduplicate membership across associations, and reconcile owner changes, coverage transfers, deactivation and expiry using the recorded grant reason. Remove only access derived from the changed assignment; preserve independently authorized grants. Stage 1 checks partner assignments when a partner is present; a partner is not required on every opportunity. Partner overlays receive access only for their assigned partner and geo/territory coverage. Broader direct-pipeline visibility needs an explicit entitlement. Team membership does not grant forecast, Amount, commercial approval, registration approval or validation-bypass rights. Recalculate derived access when coverage changes and preserve audit history. Require a nonblank explanation in Association Description / Plan when Role On Deal or Account Classification is Other. CSP includes hyperscalers; retain the current classification set. Marketplace-specific categories remain deferred. Retain the documented partner contribution rules until an approved replacement is implemented; no automatic sunset. Keep original attribution evidence, management override audit and booked-credit freeze. Partner associations may be added throughout the lifecycle; association after booking does not rewrite booked credit and partner presence is not a universal stage gate.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.

After an association becomes inactive, preserve history and stop requiring new engagement input. Status choices and the Opportunity API mapping must be verified before enabling this rule; do not substitute the association Active/Inactive status.

All ongoing activities ↑

Automations and permissions

Sales, Sales Engineering and assigned NVIDIA partner coverage maintain permitted engagement information; Partner Ops owns account coverage. Existing relevance and status validation remains.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.

Proposed field; no current input control. Use the proposed type shown above.

No existing field confirmed; proposed capture.New fieldShow and require only when OK to Interview = No. Hide or disable otherwise and never validate this hidden input. Reuse the corresponding Win/Loss rollout field if already deployed; confirm mapping before creating a field.

Win wire content and distribution

Strategic outcome; the win; business impact; technical and operational impact; why DDN won; strategic implications; team recognition; final takeaway. Use the customer, amount and strategic outcome in the subject.

Deal Team Reports and interview summaries remain restricted. The reviewed Win wire is for internal GTM; external reuse requires separate approval. CI selects interviews weekly; the AE provides an introduction within two business days when requested. Interviews do not delay reports.

Win/Loss rules for this design: ≥$1M requires Resources Engaged; >$1M triggers Win wire; >$2M adds the full Win Report. Capture at entry to Won decision (6), distinct from booking at 7. Include Renewals in interview capture, keep Competitor Lost To loss-only, capture competition in Stage 2 and include Relationship once in Won Reason.

Operating rules

At entry to Won decision (6), require Won Reason and Business Value with specific win-reason detail. Carry forward Technical Value and Operational Value. For amounts ≥ $1M also require Resources Engaged. Capture interview eligibility for New Business, Existing Business and Renewal: Yes requires an interview Contact; No requires WL Reason Cannot Approach. Above $1M notify the Sales Manager and copy the AE; manager submits the reviewed Win wire to Enablement / #win-wire within two business days. Above $2M also require the Deal Team Win Report. Historical closed records are not retro-validated.

All ongoing activities ↑

Automations and permissions

Sales maintains existing win inputs; existing internal recipients and recognition scope remain. Sales Ops coordinates corrections within scope.

Access: role permissions and automation controls.
